Sponsor's analyses

1.3.1. Survival analysis
The statistical analyses for mortality of main study animals, excluding the positive control
group (Group 6), were performed separately for each sex. The survival function of each
group was estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method (SAS Institute, 2016)
applied on daily intervals and graphs was produced. Any animal with accidental injury that
causes its death or its unscheduled sacrifice, or still alive at the day following the last day of
the experimental period was censored in the estimation.
The log-rank test was applied to Groups 1 to 5 in order to assess the significance of the
overall group effect on mortality data. If this test is found to be significant, then the

Reference ID: 5558473

NDA217673

Page 16 of 47

significance of a dose-related trend in mortality across the vehicle control groups (Group 1)
and the three test article treated groups (Groups 3, 4, and 5), and across the water control
group (Group 2) and Groups 3, 4, and 5 was evaluated using Peto’s two-sided test (Peto,
1980). In addition, the mortality rate in each of Groups 1 and 2 was compared to the one in
each of Groups 3, 4, and 5, using Peto’s two-sided test.
All statistical analyses were performed via SAS (SAS Institute, 2016), and all statistical tests
were conducted at the 5% significance level.
Sponsor’s findings: The sponsor’s analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of survival
were 24 (96%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%) and 9 (90%) in male mice, and 24
(96%), 23 (92%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), 23 (92%) and 10 (100%) in female mice in vehicle,
water control, low, medium, high and positive dose groups, respectively.
The sponsor concluded that, survival statistical analysis did not find any overall doserelated trend in mortality across all groups

1.3.2. Tumor data analysis
The statistical evaluation of tumor main study data, excluding the positive control group
(Group 6), was done separately for each sex and was limited to subcutis and
hemolymphoreticular tissue using all study animals, to all non-secondary neoplastic lesions
found in study planrequired tissues/sites, and to the combination of hemangiosarcoma
findings across whole body.
For each dataset of interest within each sex, the significance of an overall linear doserelated increase in tumor incidence rate, across Groups 1, 3, 4, and 5, and across Groups 2,
3, 4, and 5 was evaluated using Cochran-Armitage’s one-sided exact test. Furthermore,
each of Groups 1 and 2 was compared to each of Groups 3, 4, and 5. These pairwise
comparisons were implemented using Fisher’s exact one-sided test, in order to check if the
tumor incidence rate in each of Groups 3, 4, and 5 is significantly higher than the tumor
incidence rate in Groups 1 and 2.

Reference ID: 5558473

NDA217673

Page 17 of 47

All statistical analyses were performed via SAS (SAS Institute, 2016), and all statistical tests
will be conducted at the 5% significance level.
Sponsor’s findings: The sponsor concluded that no clear carcinogenic effect related to
INS1007 administration was observed at any dose level in this study.

1.4.

Reviewer's analyses

To verify sponsor’s analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing
pharmacologist, this reviewer independently performed survival and tumor data analyses.

1.4.1. Survival analysis
The survival distributions of three treated groups, one vehical control group, and one positive
control group were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. The dose
response relationship in survival was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity
of survival distributions was tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for
survival rates are given in Figures 3 and 4 in the appendix for male and female mice,
respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 18 and 19 in the appendix for
male and female mice, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and
homogeneity of survivals among the vehicle control/water control, and the treated groups, and
pairwise comparisons of vehicle control/water control with each of the treated group and
positive control goroup are given in Tables 20 to 23 in the appendix for male and female mice,
respectively.
Reviewer’s findings: This reviewer’s analysis showed that the numbers (percents) of
survival were 24 (96%), 24 (96%), 24 (96%), 25 (100%), 24 (96%) and 9 (90%) in male mice,
and 24 (96%), 23 (92%), 25 (100%), 25 (100%), 23 (92%) and 10 (100%) in female mice in
vehicle control, water control, low, medium, high and positive dose groups, respectively.
The survival analyses did not show any statistically significant dose response relationship in
mortality across the vehicle/water control groups and treated groups for either males or
females. The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in
mortality between the vehicle/water control group and any of the treated groups in either

Reference ID: 5558473

NDA217673

Page 18 of 47

males or females.
The pairwise comparisons did not show any statistically significant differences in mortality
between the vehicle/water control group and the positive control group in either males or
females.

1.4.2. Tumor data analysis
The reviewer used the same tumor data analysis methods for the mouse tumor data
analysis as they used for rat tumor data analysis.
The tumor rates and the p-values for the positive dose response relationship tests and
pairwise comparisons between water/vehicle control and the treated groups, and between
water/vehicle and positive control are listed in Tables 24 to 31 in the appendix for male and
female mice, respectively.
Adjustment for multiple testing: For the chronic study in rats, the adjustment of multiple
testing of the dose response relationship for a submission with one chronic rat study and
one chronic mouse study, the more recently revised draft (January, 2013) FDA guidance for
the carcinogenicity studies suggests the use of test levels α =0.005 for common tumors
and α=0.025 for rare tumors for the chronic mouse study. For pairwise comparisonsfor the
chronic rat study in the above type of submission with one chronic rat study and one chronic
mouse study, the same guidance document suggests the use of test levels α =0.01 for
common tumors and α =0.05 for rare tumors for the chronic mouse study.
It should be noted that the FDA guidance for multiple testing for dose response relationship
is based on a publication by Lin and Rahman (1998). In this work the authors investigated
the use of this rule for Peto analysis. However, in a later work Rahman and Lin (2008)
showed that this rule for multiple testing for dose response relationship is also suitable for
Poly-K tests.
Reviewer’s findings: The tumor types in Tables 6 to 11 below showed p-values less than or
equal to 0.05 in the tests between vehicle control and the treated groups, water control and

Reference ID: 5558473

NDA217673

Page 19 of 47

the treated groups, vehicle control and positive control and water control and positive group
for males and females.
Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed in the mouse data
analysis section, we make the following statistical conclusions:
1.

For male mice, the trend test showed a statistically significant positive dose
response relationships in the incidence of hemangiosarcoma in spleen (pvalue=0.0257<0.05) across the vehicle control and the treated groups.

2.

For female mice, the trend test showed a statistically significant positive dose
response relationships in the incidence of hemangiosarcoma in spleen (pvalue=0.0245<0.05) across the water control and the treated groups.

3.

For male mice, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the
positive control showed statistically significant increases in the incidences of
bronchioloalveolar adenoma in lung, (p-value<0.001), combined tumors of
bronchioloalveolar adenoma and carcinoma in lung, (p-value<0.001),
hemangiosarcoma in spleen (p-value=0.0085<0.05), and combined tumors of
hemangiosarcoma in whole body (p-value=0.0085<0.05).

4.

For male mice, the pairwise comparisons between the water control and the
positive control showed statistically significant increases in the incidences of
bronchioloalveolar adenoma in lung, (p-value<0.001), combined tumors of
bronchioloalveolar adenoma and carcinoma in lung, (p-value<0.001),
hemangiosarcoma in spleen (p-value=0.0079<0.05), and combined tumors of
hemangiosarcoma in whole body (p-value=0.0232<0.05).

5.

For female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the vehicle control and the
positive control showed statistically significant increases in the incidence of
bronchioloalveolar adenoma in lung, (p-value<0.001).

6.

For female mice, the pairwise comparisons between the water control and the
positive control showed statistically significant increases in the incidence of
bronchioloalveolar adenoma in lung, (p-value<0.001).
